#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

typedef long long ll;
typedef unsigned long long ull;

const ull BASE = 911382629;
const ull MOD = 1e18 + 3;

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(0);

    string s, t;
    cin >> s >> t;
    int n = s.size();
    int m = t.size();

    vector<int> prefix_0(n + 1, 0), prefix_1(n + 1, 0);
    vector<int> zero_indices, one_indices;
    int first_zero = -1, first_one = -1;

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        prefix_0[i + 1] = prefix_0[i] + (s[i] == '0');
        prefix_1[i + 1] = prefix_1[i] + (s[i] == '1');
        if (s[i] == '0') {
            zero_indices.push_back(i);
            if (first_zero == -1) first_zero = i;
        } else {
            one_indices.push_back(i);
            if (first_one == -1) first_one = i;
        }
    }

    int count0 = prefix_0[n];
    int count1 = prefix_1[n];

    if (count0 + count1 > m) {
        cout << "0\n";
        return 0;
    }

    vector<ull> hash_t(m + 1, 0);
    vector<ull> power(m + 1, 1);
    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        hash_t[i + 1] = (hash_t[i] * BASE + t[i]) % MOD;
        power[i + 1] = (power[i] * BASE) % MOD;
    }

    auto get_hash = [&](int l, int r) -> ull {
        if (l > r) return 0;
        ull res = (hash_t[r + 1] - hash_t[l] * power[r - l + 1]) % MOD;
        return res;
    };

    int ans = 0;

    for (int len0 = 1; len0 <= m; ++len0) {
        if (count0 * len0 >= m) break;
        ll remaining = m - count0 * len0;
        if (remaining % count1 != 0) continue;
        int len1 = remaining / count1;
        if (len1 < 1) continue;

        // Check if first 0's substring is possible
        if (len0 > m) continue;
        ull r0_hash = get_hash(0, len0 - 1);

        // Find first 1 in s and its start position
        int first_one_pos = first_one;
        if (first_one_pos == -1) continue; // s has no 1s, but problem states there is at least one 0 and one 1
        ll start_first_one = (ll)prefix_0[first_one_pos] * len0 + (ll)prefix_1[first_one_pos] * len1;
        ll end_first_one = start_first_one + len1 - 1;
        if (end_first_one >= m) continue;
        ull r1_hash = get_hash(start_first_one, end_first_one);

        if (r0_hash == r1_hash) continue;

        bool valid = true;

        // Check all zeros
        for (int idx : zero_indices) {
            ll start = (ll)prefix_0[idx] * len0 + (ll)prefix_1[idx] * len1;
            ll end = start + len0 - 1;
            if (end >= m) { valid = false; break; }
            ull curr_hash = get_hash(start, end);
            if (curr_hash != r0_hash) { valid = false; break; }
        }
        if (!valid) continue;

        // Check all ones
        for (int idx : one_indices) {
            ll start = (ll)prefix_0[idx] * len0 + (ll)prefix_1[idx] * len1;
            ll end = start + len1 - 1;
            if (end >= m) { valid = false; break; }
            ull curr_hash = get_hash(start, end);
            if (curr_hash != r1_hash) { valid = false; break; }
        }
        if (valid) ans++;
    }

    cout << ans << "\n";
    return 0;
}<#sep#> +x3x4 is negative, so RHS is positive. Then, can we make LHS smaller than RHS?

Wait, if q is negative, then the inequality is x1² + x2² +x3² +x4² ≥ q*(something). If q is negative, the RHS is q*(something). If the something is positive, then RHS is negative, so LHS ≥ negative number, which is always true. If the something is negative, then RHS is positive (since q is negative and something is negative, product is positive). So in that case, we need LHS ≥ positive number. But since x_i can be any real numbers, we can choose x_i such that x1x2 +x2x3 +x3x4 is a large negative number, making RHS a large positive number. But if we set x_i such that LHS is small, then we might get LHS < RHS. For example, let’s try q = -1. Let’s pick x1 = x2 = x3 = x4 = t. Then LHS = 4t², RHS = -1*( t² + t² + t² ) = -3t². So inequality becomes 4t² ≥ -3t², which is always true since t² is non-negative. But in this case, RHS is negative. To get a case where RHS is positive when q is negative, we need x1x2 +x2x3 +x3x4 to be negative. Let’s choose x1 = 1, x2 = -1, x3 = 1, x4 = -1. Then x1x2 +x2x3 +x3x4 = (1)(-1) + (-1)(1) + (1)(-1) = -1 -1 -1 = -3. So RHS = q*(-3). If q is negative, then RHS is positive. Let’s take q = -2. Then RHS = (-2)*(-3) = 6. The LHS is 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 = 4. So 4 ≥ 6? That's false. Therefore, when q is negative, the inequality does not hold. So q must be non-negative. So q ≤ 4/3 and q ≥ 0. But perhaps even within 0 ≤ q ≤ 4/3, there might be tighter constraints. Let's check.

Wait, actually in the case where q is positive, the main constraint is when the RHS is positive. So we need to consider when x1x2 + x2x3 +x3x4 is positive, because when it's negative, RHS is negative, and LHS is non-negative, so inequality holds. So to find the maximal q, we need to consider the cases where the sum x1x2 +x2x3 +x3x4 is positive.

Alternatively, maybe rewrite the quadratic form to make it a sum of squares. Let me try to group terms.

The expression is:

x1² + x2² + x3² + x4² - q x1x2 - q x2x3 - q x3x4 ≥ 0.

Maybe group x1 and x2, x2 and x3, x3 and x4.

Start by completing the square for x1 and x2:

x1² - q x1x2 + x2² = (x1 - (q/2)x2)^2 + x2² - (q²/4)x2².

Wait, that's how completing the square works. So for the terms involving x1 and x2:

x1² - q x1x2 + x2² = (x1 - (q/2)x2)^2 + x2²(1 - q²/4).

Similarly, proceed for other terms. But the problem is the x2 term is also involved in the next cross term with x3.

So perhaps this approach will not work as neatly, since x2 is connected to both x1 and x3.

Another approach is to use variables in pairs. For example, let me set variables such that the cross terms can be handled. Let's see:

Let me consider variables x1, x2, x3, x4. The cross terms are x1x2, x2x3, x3x4. So it's a chain: x1 connected to x2, x2 connected to x3, x3 connected to x4. So perhaps using variables y1 = x1, y2 = x2, y3 = x3, y4 = x4, but arranged in a way to diagonalize the quadratic form.

Alternatively, think of the quadratic form as a combination of vectors. Let me consider writing the inequality as:

||x||² ≥ q(x1x2 + x2x3 +x3x4).

We can use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality for the RHS. Let's see:

x1x2 +x2x3 +x3x4 ≤ |x1x2| + |x2x3| + |x3x4|.

But then using AM ≤ (a² + b²)/2 for each term:

|x1x2| ≤ (x1² + x2²)/2,

|x2x3| ≤ (x2² + x3²)/2,

|x3x4| ≤ (x3² + x4²)/2.

Adding these up:

|x1x2| + |x2x3| + |x3x4| ≤ (x1² + x2² + x2² + x3² + x3² + x4²)/2 = (x1² + 2x2² + 2x3² + x4²)/2.

Therefore, x1x2 + x2x3 +x3x4 ≤ (x1² + 2x2² + 2x3² + x4²)/2.

So substituting into the original inequality:

LHS = x1² + x2² +x3² +x4² ≥ q*(something) ≤ q*( (x1² + 2x2² + 2x3² + x4²)/2 )

Therefore, to make sure that x1² +x2² +x3² +x4² ≥ q*( (x1² + 2x2² + 2x3² +x4²)/2 )

This would require:

(1 - q/2)x1² + (1 - q) x2² + (1 - q)x3² + (1 - q/2)x4² ≥ 0.

For this to hold for all real numbers, each coefficient must be non-negative. Therefore:

1 - q/2 ≥ 0 ⇒ q ≤ 2,

1 - q ≥ 0 ⇒ q ≤ 1,

Similarly for x3²: same as x2², so 1 - q ≥ 0 ⇒ q ≤1,

And for x4²: same as x1², 1 - q/2 ≥0 ⇒ q ≤ 2.

Therefore, the most restrictive condition is q ≤1. But earlier, when setting all variables equal, we found q ≤4/3. Hmmm, so there's a discrepancy here. Because if using Cauchy-Schwarz gives a bound of q ≤1, but the case with all variables equal allows q up to 4/3. This suggests that the Cauchy-Schwarz approach gives a weaker bound (lower upper limit on q) than the actual maximum. Therefore, the Cauchy-Schwarz approach is too conservative here.

Therefore, this method might not give the tightest possible bound. So perhaps another approach is needed.

Let me try to use Lagrange multipliers. Suppose we want to find the maximum q such that x1² +x2² +x3² +x4² - q(x1x2 +x2x3 +x3x4) ≥0 for all real x_i. The minimal value of the expression (LHS - RHS) must be ≥0. The minimum occurs when the gradient is zero. Let's set up the function:

f(x1, x2, x3, x4) = x1² +x2² +x3² +x4² - q(x1x2 +x2x3 +x3x4).

Taking partial derivatives and setting them to zero:

∂f/∂x1 = 2x1 - q x2 = 0,

∂f/∂x2 = 2x2 - q x1 - q x3 = 0,

∂f/∂x3 = 2x3 - q x2 - q x4 = 0,

∂f/∂x4 = 2x4 - q x3 = 0.

This system of equations can be written as:

1. 2x1 = q x2,

2. 2x2 =<#sep#> 10 (radii: fire radius is 10, freeze also 10, etc. But with 0 freezes. 

So, the freeze_count is 0, so D = ∫_{x} F(x)* 1 dx.

Which is exactly the fire tower's coverage interval integral. Because 1×F.

The fire tower's coverage is around its center. To get maximum integral, the tower has to be placed such that the length of its interval is maximum. Because its damage is df=100, but coverage's length is 2*sqrt(10² -1) = 2*sqrt(99) ≈ 19.8997. So the total damage is 100 multiplied by that number → the sample output is 100 × (2×sqrt(99)), which is 1989.9748… which matches the first sample's output. So yes that makes sense.

Second sample input:

1 fire, 0 electric, 1 freezing.

Radii 10 for all.

df=100.

Freeze radius is rs=10 also, so the freeze's R_s is sqrt(100–1) = same as fire.

So:

Place the freeze tower to overlap with the fire tower.

If you place both fire and freeze towers at x=0, the freeze owner is two towers (but you have only 1 freeze allowed. Oh wait, no, the count is ns=1. So we can place the freeze tower either on (0,1) or (0,-1), but since fire is placed at some position, maybe at (0,1), then the freeze must be placed elsewhere? Wait maybe.

Wait in second case: the two towers must be placed on different positions, but can they be same x as other types?

Yes, such as fire and freeze tower can be placed on same x, but different y. They can't share the same (x,y).

Thus, the optimal arrangement would be placing both towers (fire and freeze) on same x-coordinate (e.g., 0), so their coverage intervals coincide. The freeze is one tower (since ns=1). But then, when the freeze is placed at (0, -1), the fire is placed at (0,+1). 

Thus their coverage intervals for the fire and freeze are exactly the same (since the radial distance equations are same).

Thus the freeze tower contributes 1 to K(x) in their common interval. 

Thus K(x) =1 wherever in the interval. 

The fire's damage is 100, so the damage is 100 * (1+1) * length of coverage region. 

Thus the integral is 200 * (2*sqrt(10^2 -1)) → which is 200 × 19.8997… gives 3979.949… which matches sample.

So the key was to have the freeze overlapping the fire's region, and both maximized timing.

Thus, the strategy in that sample is to overlap as much as possible.

Hence, the plan is to place as many freeze towers as possible to overlap with as many fire and electric's coverage as possible, while arranging fire and electric in that same central region.

Now, perhaps the way to compute this is:

For any tower placement (fire/electric), their best is to be centered around a point covered by as many freeze towers as possible.

Thus, the central point would be chosen so that as many freeze towers as possible can be placed in such a way that their combined coverage intervals have the highest possible freeze counts (K) in the covered region.

To achieve that, the freeze region must be arranged as tightly as possible.

Thus here’s an approach:

1. Determine the best central position for all freeze towers. E.g., distribute them across some central interval (like x= x0 to x0 +n) spaced tightly to maximize coverage overlap.

2. Then, place all fire and electric towers in such a way their position centers are within the freeze's coverage regions so their damage is multiplied by the high K.

To model this:

First, suppose that freeze towers are placed in the most efficient way. Let's assume that freezing towers are arranged in a minimal span to maximize K(x) in the central point.

For each freezers, the ideal is to have them as close to each other as possible, in a 1-unit spaced grid, in order to have their coverage maximally overlapping.

If they can be placed all at positions x = a such that the coverage regions all overlap at their center ?

Wait when you have multiple freeze towers arranged around the central x-coordinate, each at x = x_center ± d, etc.

Thus, the best way is to place all freeze towers in a block around some central x, for that, you choose a central x (like zero?), and arrange all freezes towers as close to that x as possible.

Then the maximum K(x) will be at the center of that block. 

Once that block is chosen, fire and electric can be placed such that their center is within the central region of freezes' block.

So to maximize the fire tower coverage in regions of maximum freeze count (K). 

Thus, the main steps in thinking algorithmically would be:

a. Find the optimal freeze configuration that maximizes K(x) over some region where it's possible to place fire/electric towers for high damage.

b. Given that freeze configuration, place fire and electric in their coverage regions to cover the intervals where K(x) is highest. 

c. Compare different possible freeze positions and configurations to find the best D.

However, this requires considering multiple possibilities for freeze configurations and comparing their performance. Given the problem constraints (nf + ne + ns <=20), perhaps it's feasible by evaluating all possible freeze tower arrangements around different centers, for small numbers?

Alternatively, perhaps the optimal configuration can be determined as follows:

For best overlapping, this block should be arranged so that the distance between their centers is less than or equal to 2L (so their coverage regions overlap with every other tower).

Wait To make sure each adjacent pair of freezing towers' intervals overlaps, the distance between them should be <= 2*L.

The spacing between their x centers is 1, so to ensure that 1 <= 2*L → L >=0.5. Since L is from freezing radius squared (since L= sqrt(rs^2-1)), rs >= sqrt( (0.5)^2+1 ), but rs >=1 anyway, which satisfies this.

So, freezing towers placed consecutively from a to b will have overlapping coverage between each adjacent pair. Thus, the central part of this block has high coverage (most freeze towers around that region), while the extremal regions have fewer.

Thus the optimal block placement is to have all freeze towers placed consecutively in the smallest possible interval (to maximize their overlapping in that central region).

Hence the freeze width is determined by their count: ns. For example, putting them in a block of length ns, but since each x can have two freeze towers, perhaps that allows more?

Thus, given ns freezes, it's best to use as many as possible two per x, formed into a block centered around point.

Hence, for the optimal freeze configuration:

Place at x=2 (but partially filled).

The best way is:

number of full x's with two freeze towers: suppose ns divided by 2.

So num_full = floor( ns / 2 ).

The excess is remainder.

Hence, first arrange the num_full towers on x positions starting from some center, then place the excess one on the next position.

The center for freezing should be at the middle<#sep#>BUG DetectorReader misses detectors that start with and contain identical names
## Summary of issue
A colleague has a detector file with the following energy dependent detectors: `spectrum`, `spectrumA`, and `spectrumB`. The DetectorReader fails to capture the later two detectors, and messes up the data stored on `spectrum`.

## Code for reproducing the issue
Process a detector file with the above names. One has been created for reproducibility using the `bwr_det0.m` file and the following commands
```sh
$ sed "s/spectrum/spectrumA/" bwr_det0.m > a
$ sed "s/spectrum/spectrumB/" bwr_det0.m > b
$ cat bwr_det0.m a b > joined_det0.m
```
Process with python
```python
>>> import serpentTools
>>> det = serpentTools.read("joined_det0.m")
```
## Actual outcome including console output and error traceback if applicable
```python
>>> det.detectors
{'spectrum' : <serpentTools.detector.Detector object at ...>}
>>> det["spectrum"].grids.keys()
dict_keys(["E", "A", "AE", "B", "BE"])
```
## Expected outcome
All three detectors are properly defined.

## Versions

Please provide the following:

* Version  from ``serpentTools.__version__`` `0.9.1`
* Python version - ``python --version`` - `3.7`

## Likely culprit
https://github.com/CORE-GATECH-GROUP/serpent-tools/blob/2dc5a9d03bc510d358032f270575f0ab3120584f/serpentTools/parsers/detector.py#L63

# Workaround

If the file has already been produced, then users can change the detector names to be more distinct, e.g.
```sh
$ sed -e "s/spectrumA/Aspectrum/" -e "s/spectrumB/Bspectrum/" joined_det0.m > mod_det0.m
```
then serpentTools reveals that `spectrum`, `Aspectrum`, and `Bspectrum` detectors have been found.

If the detector names must be present in the file, users can put the longer names first, or break up the ordering with other detectors. This will "confuse" the matching. Putting `spectrum` last yields the correct detectors.

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct WordInfo {
    int r_count;
    int length;
};

struct Component {
    WordInfo best_in_component;
    WordInfo overall_best;
    vector<int> adj_components;
};

vector<Component> components;
vector<int> component_id;
vector<vector<int>> adj;
vector<vector<int>> adj_rev;
vector<WordInfo> word_info;
vector<string> words;
unordered_map<string, int> word_to_id;

int get_id(const string &word) {
    auto it = word_to_id.find(word);
    if (it != word_to_id.end()) {
        return it->second;
    }
    int id = word_to_id.size();
    word_to_id[word] = id;
    words.push_back(word);
    int r = 0;
    for (char c : word) {
        if (tolower(c) == 'r') {
            r++;
        }
    }
    word_info.push_back({r, (int)word.length()});
    return id;
}

void dfs1(int u, vector<bool>& visited, vector<int>& order) {
    stack<int> st;
    st.push(u);
    while (!st.empty()) {
        int v = st.top();
        if (visited[v]) {
            st.pop();
            order.push_back(v);
            continue;
        }
        visited[v] = true;
        for (int w : adj[v]) {
            if (!visited[w]) {
                st.push(w);
            }
        }
    }
}

void dfs2(int u, int component, vector<bool>& visited) {
    stack<int> st;
    st.push(u);
    visited[u] = true;
    component_id[u] = component;
    while (!st.empty()) {
        int v = st.top();
        st.pop();
        for (int w : adj_rev[v]) {
            if (!visited[w]) {
                visited[w] = true;
                component_id[w] = component;
                st.push(w);
            }
        }
    }
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int m;
    cin >> m;
    vector<string> essay;
    string s;
    cin.ignore();
    getline(cin, s);
    istringstream iss(s);
    for (int i = 0; i < m; i++) {
        string word;
        iss >> word;
        essay.push_back(word);
    }

    int n;
    cin >> n;
    cin.ignore();
    adj.clear();
    adj_rev.clear();
    word_to_id.clear();
    word_info.clear();
    words.clear();

    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        string x, y;
        cin >> x >> y;
        transform(x.begin(), x.end(), x.begin(), ::tolower);
        transform(y.begin(), y.end(), y.begin(), ::tolower);
        int u = get_id(x);
        int v = get_id(y);
        if (u >= adj.size()) adj.resize(u + 1);
        if (v >= adj.size()) adj.resize(v + 1);
        adj[u].push_back(v);
        if (v >= adj_rev.size()) adj_rev.resize(v + 1);
        if (u >= adj_rev.size()) adj_rev.resize(u + 1);
        adj_rev[v].push_back(u);
    }

    int num_nodes = word_to_id.size();
    adj.resize(num_nodes);
    adj_rev.resize(num_nodes);

    vector<bool> visited(num_nodes, false);
    vector<int> order;
    for (int i = 0; i < num_nodes; i++) {
        if (!visited[i]) {
            dfs1(i, visited, order);
        }
    }

    reverse(order.begin(), order.end());
    fill(visited.begin(), visited.end(), false);
    component_id.assign(num_nodes, -1);
    int component_count = 0;
    components.clear();

    for (int u : order) {
        if (!visited[u]) {
            components.emplace_back();
            dfs2(u, component_count, visited);
            component_count++;
        }
    }

    for (Component &comp : components) {
        WordInfo best = {INT_MAX, INT_MAX};
        for (int u = 0; u < num_nodes; u++) {
            if (component_id[u] == &comp - &components[0]) {
                const WordInfo &info = word_info[u];
                if (info.r_count < best.r_count || (info.r_count == best.r_count && info.length < best.length)) {
                    best = info;
                }
            }
        }
        comp.best_in_component = best;
        comp.overall_best = best;
    }

    vector<unordered_set<int>> dag_edges(components.size());
    for (int u = 0; u < num_nodes; u++) {
        for (int v : adj[u]) {
            int cu = component_id[u];
            int cv = component_id[v];
            if (cu != cv) {
                dag_edges[cu].insert(cv);
            }
        }
    }

    vector<vector<int>> dag_adj(components.size());
    for (int i = 0; i < components.size(); i++) {
        dag_adj[i].assign(dag_edges[i].begin(), dag_edges[i].end());
    }

    vector<int> topo_order(components.size());
    iota(topo_order.begin(), topo_order.end(), 0);
    reverse(topo_order.begin(), topo_order.end());

    for (int i : topo_order) {
        Component &comp = components[i];
        WordInfo current_best = comp.best_in_component;
        for (int neighbor : dag_adj[i]) {
            const WordInfo &nb = components[neighbor].overall_best;
            if (nb.r_count < current_best.r_count || (nb.r_count == current_best.r_count && nb.length < current_best.length)) {
                current_best = nb;
            }
        }
        comp.overall_best = current_best;
    }

    long long total_r = 0, total_len = 0;
    for (const string &original_word : essay) {
        string word = original_word;
        transform(word.begin(), word.end(), word.begin(), ::tolower);
        auto it = word_to_id.find(word);
        if (it == word_to_id.end()) {
            int r = 0, len = word.length();
            for (char c : word) {
                if (tolower(c) == 'r') r++;
            }
            total_r += r;
            total_len += len;
        } else {
            int cid = component_id[it->second];
            total_r += components[cid].overall_best.r_count;
            total_len += components[cid].overall_best.length;
        }
    }

    cout << total_r << " " << total_len << endl;

    return 0;
}<#sep#>x3 +x3x4).

Taking partial derivatives and setting them to zero:

∂f/∂x1 = 2x1 - q x2 = 0,

∂f/∂x2 = 2x2 - q x1 - q x3 = 0,

∂f/∂x3 = 2x3 - q x2 - q x4 = 0,

∂f/∂x4 = 2x4 - q x3 = 0.

This system of equations can be written as:

1. 2x1 = q x2,

2. 2x2 = q x1 + q x3,

3. 2x3 = q x2 + q x4,

4. 2x4 = q x3.

Assuming that the minimal value is achieved at some non-trivial solution (not all x_i zero). Let's try to express all variables in terms of x1.

From equation 1: x2 = (2/q)x1,

From equation 4: x4 = (q/2)x3,

From equation 3: 2x3 = q x2 + q x4. Substitute x2 and x4:

2x3 = q*(2/q)x1 + q*(q/2)x3 ⇒ 2x3 = 2x1 + (q²/2)x3.

Rearranged: 2x3 - (q²/2)x3 = 2x1 ⇒ x3*(2 - q²/2) = 2x1 ⇒ x3 = (2x1)/(2 - q²/2) = (4x1)/(4 - q²).

Now, from equation 2: 2x2 = q x1 + q x3. Substitute x2 and x3:

2*(2/q)x1 = q x1 + q*(4x1)/(4 - q²)

Simplify left side: (4/q)x1 = q x1 + (4 q x1)/(4 - q²)

Divide both sides by x1 (assuming x1 ≠ 0):

4/q = q + (4 q)/(4 - q²)

Multiply both sides by q(4 - q²):

4(4 - q²) = q²(4 - q²) + 4 q²

Expand left side: 16 -4q²

Right side: 4q² - q^4 +4q² = 8q² - q^4

Thus:

16 -4q² = 8q² - q^4

Bring all terms to left side:

q^4 -12q² +16 =0

Let y = q², then equation becomes:

y² -12y +16 =0

Solve quadratic: y = [12 ± sqrt(144 -64)]/2 = [12 ± sqrt(80)]/2 = [12 ± 4*sqrt(5)]/2 =6 ± 2*sqrt(5)

Thus, q² =6 +2√5 or 6 -2√5

But since q is real, these are acceptable. But sqrt(6 +2√5) and sqrt(6 -2√5). Compute sqrt(6 +2√5):

Note that (√5 +1)^2 =5 +2√5 +1=6 +2√5. So sqrt(6 +2√5)=√5 +1.

Similarly, sqrt(6 -2√5). Let's see: (√5 -1)^2=5 -2√5 +1=6 -2√5. So sqrt(6 -2√5)=√5 -1.

Thus, q=±(√5 +1) or ±(√5 -1). However, since we are looking for the maximum q such that the inequality holds, we need to consider the positive solutions because if q is positive, the RHS can be positive, requiring a tighter bound. The negative q's would have different constraints, but earlier we saw that negative q can fail when RHS is positive. However, based on the Lagrange multiplier method, these critical points occur at q=√5 +1≈3.236 and q=√5 -1≈1.236. But earlier when we set all variables equal, we found q≤4/3≈1.333, which is larger than √5 -1≈1.236. So this suggests that the minimal value occurs at q=√5 -1≈1.236. But wait, the equation came from setting the gradient to zero, so the minimal value of f is zero when q satisfies the equation. Therefore, for q such that these solutions exist, the expression f(x1, x2, x3, x4) can reach zero. Therefore, to ensure that f is always non-negative, q must be less than or equal to the minimal such q where f reaches zero. But the solutions from the Lagrange multipliers give us possible q where f can be zero. The minimal positive q would be √5 -1, but in our earlier test case, setting all variables equal required q ≤4/3≈1.333, which is greater than √5 -1≈1.236. Wait, perhaps the maximum q for which f is non-negative for all x_i is the minimal such q where the equation q^4 -12 q^2 +16=0, which gives q²=6±2√5, so q=√(6+2√5)=√5 +1≈3.236 and q=√(6 -2√5)=√5 -1≈1.236. Since we're looking for the maximal q such that the inequality holds, we need to pick the smaller positive solution, because if q is too large, the inequality fails. Wait, let's check.

Wait, when we set all variables equal, q can be up to 4/3≈1.333. But √5 -1≈1.236, which is less than 4/3. Therefore, this suggests that the Lagrange multiplier method gives a lower bound for q. But this is contradictory. Alternatively, perhaps the solutions from the Lagrange multipliers are where the minimal value is zero, so for q beyond that value, the minimal value becomes negative, making the inequality invalid. Therefore, the maximal q for which the inequality holds is the smaller positive solution from the quartic equation. Let's compute q=√(6 -2√5). As we saw, sqrt(6 -2√5)=sqrt( (sqrt5 -1)^2 )=sqrt5 -1≈2.236 -1≈1.236.

Wait, sqrt5≈2.236, so sqrt5 -1≈1.236. But 4/3≈1.333. So 1.236<1.333, so according to the Lagrange multiplier method, q should be at most sqrt5 -1≈1.236. But our earlier test case allowed q up to 4/3≈1.333. So there's a conflict here. Which one is correct?

Let me test q=√5 -1≈1.236 in the case where all variables are equal. Let q=√5 -1. Then 3q≈3*(1.236)=3.708, and 4≥3q≈3.708? 4≈3.708? No, 4 is larger than 3.708. So if we set all variables equal, the inequality would hold. But according to Lagrange multipliers, q=√5 -1 is the maximum q where the minimal value of the expression is zero, meaning that for q larger than that, there exists some x_i where the expression is negative. Therefore, perhaps the case where all variables equal is not the one that gives the tightest bound. For example, if we take another set of x_i, maybe the minimal value is achieved at some other combination.

Alternatively, let's try to plug in q=√5 -1 into the original inequality and check if it holds. Let's take the non-trivial solution found by the Lagrange multipliers.

From the system above, we can find the ratios of x1, x2, x3, x4. Let's compute x1 in terms of x1:

From earlier, x2=(2/q)x1,

x3=(4x1)/(4 - q²),

x4=(q/2)x3=(q/2)*(4x1)/(4 - q²)= (2 q x1)/(4 - q²)

Let’s compute for q=√5 -1. Let's compute q²:

q=√5 -1≈1.236,

q²=(√5 -1)^2=5 -2√5 +1=6 -2√5≈6 -4.472≈1.528.

Then 4 - q²≈4 -1.528≈2<#sep#>:

x = 0, 1 (if 5 mod 2 is 1). So x=0 has 2 freezes, x=1 has 2 freezes, then need one more.

Place at x=2 (but partially filled).

Wait calculation:

The best way is:

number of full x's with two freeze towers: suppose ns divided by 2.

So num_full = floor( ns / 2 ).

The excess is remainder.

Hence, first arrange the num_full towers on x positions starting from some center, then place the excess one on the next position.

The center for freezing should be at the middle of these x positions.

Thus for maximizing K in center, the tower center is at the middle of the

For example, if ns = 5.

num_full = 2 ( total 4), then one left.

So the spacing can be arranged as:

x=-1 (2 freezes), x=0 (2) and x=1 (1), forming a block from x=-1 to 1, centered at 0.

The freeze's center would then be at zero, and the overlap would be highest here.

Thus, the freeze's coverage for those at -1.

-1 has L = sqrt(rs²-1) → interval from -1-L to -1 + L.

The x=0's interval is 0 - L to 0 + L.

The x=1's interval (the one with one freeze tower) is 1-L to 1+ L.

Thus, the maximum coverage K(x) would be highest at x=0 where all towers within the area contribute.

Hence the maximum K there would be: between all freeze's placed between -1 to 1, plus any others.

Wait in this case, at x=0: 

It's covered by:

the freeze at x=-1 (assuming their x is nearby). The distance from -1 to 0 is 1 unit. In order for that tower to cover x=0, need the freeze's coverage radius L >=1 (since distance between centers is 1).

L <1?

If L <1, then the x =0 would be outside the coverage of the x =-1 tower.

This is problematic. 

So the question is whether the freeze's R_s (L in this case) is large enough to allow overlapping of adjacent placed towers. 

Hence, % 

The minimal R_s required for adjacent freezes (distance 1) to cover overlapping regions:

The required L must be> 0.5? Well, for consecutive towers, the coverage of the freeze at xi and the next xi+1.

The distance between the two towers is 1. 

The freeze's coverage of each is L. So the overlap between them requires that:

the right endpoint of xi's coverage reaches (xi + L).

The left endpoint of xi+1's is (xi+1 – L) = xi +1 - L.

These two must overlap → xi + L >= xi+1 – L → L >= (xi+1 - xi)/2 → L >= 0.5.

Thus, if L <0.5 (i.e., R_s < sqrt( (0.5)^2 +1)), which is R_s < sqrt(0.25 +1) = sqrt(1.25)≈1.118, then the freeze towers cannot overlap their adjacent positions.

Otherwise, If R_s has sqrt(rs²-1) >= 0.5 → rs² >= 1.25 → rs >= ~1.118.

So, depending on the radii.

But how to decide. Since we don't know the input, but the algorithm must work for any.

This complicates things. But maybe there is a general approach.

Assuming that the radii are sufficiently large (which is common in most cases), we can safely proceed with the assumption that freeze Towers spaced at adjacent x positions have overlapping regions — so the optimal freeze configuration is to place all freeze towers consecutively around the central x point.

故此，我可能需要尝试两种可能：

我需要编写一个算法，可以枚举不同的可能放置冻结塔的方式，然后计算总damage，并找到最优解。

Here's an approach outline:

The problem can be viewed as place nf + ne + ns towers in such a way:

- Each tower's position is at (x, y) where y is ±1, and x is an integer.

- fire, electric, freeze counts must satisfy constraints.

- The goal is maximize D.

Due to the small constraints (maximum total towers 20), perhaps we can represent the positions and their tower types, and compute the possible damage, then pick the maximum.

But even this approach's computation may be heavy, but perhaps manageable.

Steps for this approach:

1.枚举所有可能的 freeze塔的放置， electric塔和fire塔的放置. 

这可能不行，由于最大为20个塔， positions数目太大。

Perhaps a better approach uses the following observations:

Each tower's contribution to the integral D is the product of:

a) its own damage rate (for fire/electric) * \int_{他的 coverage区间} (1+K(x)) dx 

b) For freeze tower, it contributes to enhancing the damage in all areas where it covers (through K(x)), but no direct damage.

因此，每个火灾和電氣塔的 own damage is dependent on the path where they overlap with freezing towers.

Since the problem is to choose the tower positions (type and locations), perhaps we should focus on optimally packing fire and electric towers into the regions with highest possible freeze coverage multiplied by their own coverage time.

The key thing is to choose positions of all towers such that the overlap integrals are maximized.

This feels like a combinatorially intensive problem. But given the problem's constraint limits, perhaps branch-and-bound or some greedy method can work.

Alternatively, perhaps the optimal configuration can be found by:

1. Choosing freeze positions to form the largest possible overlapping region of their own coverage area (so maximizes K there).

2. Then, choose fire and electric positions such that they are centered around that freeze region.

Thus, the algorithm could be:

- Find the optimal freeze configuration (way to place all freeze towers)that gives the highest possible freeze level (K(x)) in some central region, then compute where to place fire and electric towers to maximally benefit from that K(x).

To find the optimal freeze configuration, it would be best to arrange freeze towers as tightly as possible (spaced 1 unit apart), such that their coverage intervals overlap as much as possible.

Assuming this arrangement is best, then the freeze region is centered at some x_center.

Once this is determined, fire and electric towers should be placed as close as possible to this x_center.

To compute this, we can:

Choose a central position (say， x_center).

Place all freeze towers as close to x_center as possible (i.e., centered positions around x_center).

Then place fire and electric towers also around that区域.

The detailed steps would be:

1. For freezes, find the tightest cluster (smallest spread x positions possible):

Let ns be the number of freezes.

Each freeze tower occupies 0 or 1 per position (since each x can contain at most 2, but ns may be even or odd).

Let the optimal freeze configuration allow for the maximum possible overlap．

The most compact arrangement is to place towers in neighboring x's starting from 0.

For example， the freezes can be placed:

• If ns <= 2:

 all at x=0.

 if ns=3: 2 at x=0, 1 at x=1.

 or 2 at 0 and one at-1 -, whichever gives more high overlap.

But this may vary.

Thus for a given ns, create the frozen positions to cluster them as fit.

Once the freeze tower positions are set (i.e. their x and y coordinates), can compute the freeze\'s coverage.

• For each x coordinate on the axis, compute the K(x) = number of freezing towers that cover x.

Once the freeze towers are fixed, then to choose the火 and electr towers positioning.

The fire and electric’s optimal positions are those x_coordinates where the K(x) is highest (as this multiplies their damage) so we want to place their towers at x centers such that their coverage intervals fall within high-K区域.

Thus, the optimal placement for a fire tower is to center it such that its coverage interval is within a region where K(x) is highest.

Thus the algorithm would:

For all possible freeze configuration choices (all ways of arranging freezes and their positions)， but this may be too computationally heavy.

Since ns can be up to 20, and for each freeze tower you have 2 choices of y （but per x位置,可以放置0到2 freezes, but with counts up to ns）．

 this may be impractical.

 An alternative approach is to assume that the best freeze configuration is to place冻结 타워 in the smallest interval possible, as tightly packed as possible around some x centr.

 Then, once that’s done, place fire and electric towers inside the freeze region's highest K areas.

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct Component {
    vector<int> part0;
    vector<int> part1;
    int a, b;
};

vector<Component> components;

int main() {
    int n, m;
    cin >> n >> m;
    int n1, n2, n3;
    cin >> n1 >> n2 >> n3;
    vector<vector<int>> adj(n + 1);
    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        int u, v;
        cin >> u >> v;
        adj[u].push_back(v);
        adj[v].push_back(u);
    }

    vector<int> color(n + 1, -1);
    bool possible = true;
    for (int u = 1; u <= n; ++u) {
        if (color[u] == -1) {
            queue<int> q;
            q.push(u);
            color[u] = 0;
            Component comp;
            comp.part0.push_back(u);
            while (!q.empty()) {
                int v = q.front();
                q.pop();
                for (int neighbor : adj[v]) {
                    if (color[neighbor] == -1) {
                        color[neighbor] = color[v] ^ 1;
                        (color[neighbor] == 0 ? comp.part0 : comp.part1).push_back(neighbor);
                        q.push(neighbor);
                    } else if (color[neighbor] == color[v]) {
                        possible = false;
                        break;
                    }
                }
                if (!possible) break;
            }
            if (!possible) break;
            comp.a = comp.part0.size();
            comp.b = comp.part1.size();
            components.push_back(comp);
        }
    }

    if (!possible) {
        cout << "NO\n";
        return 0;
    }

    int k = components.size();
    vector<vector<bool>> dp(k + 1, vector<bool>(n2 + 1, false));
    dp[0][0] = true;
    for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
        int a = components[i].a, b = components[i].b;
        for (int s = 0; s <= n2; ++s) {
            if (dp[i][s]) {
                if (s + a <= n2) dp[i + 1][s + a] = true;
                if (s + b <= n2) dp[i + 1][s + b] = true;
            }
        }
    }

    if (!dp[k][n2]) {
        cout << "NO\n";
        return 0;
    }

    vector<bool> choices(k);
    int current_sum = n2;
    for (int i = k - 1; i >= 0; --i) {
        int a = components[i].a, b = components[i].b;
        if (current_sum >= a && dp[i][current_sum - a]) {
            choices[i] = true;
            current_sum -= a;
        } else {
            choices[i] = false;
            current_sum -= b;
        }
    }

    vector<int> c_list;
    for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
        c_list.push_back(choices[i] ? components[i].b : components[i].a);
    }

    vector<bool> dp_subset(n1 + 1, false);
    dp_subset[0] = true;
    for (int c : c_list) {
        for (int s = n1; s >= c; --s) {
            if (dp_subset[s - c]) dp_subset[s] = true;
        }
    }

    if (!dp_subset[n1]) {
        cout << "NO\n";
        return 0;
    }

    vector<bool> includes(k, false);
    int s_remaining = n1;
    for (int i = k - 1; i >= 0; --i) {
        if (s_remaining >= c_list[i] && dp_subset[s_remaining - c_list[i]]) {
            includes[i] = true;
            s_remaining -= c_list[i];
        }
    }

    vector<char> label(n + 1);
    for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
        const Component &comp = components[i];
        bool choice = choices[i];
        bool include = includes[i];
        char col = include ? '1' : '3';
        if (choice) {
            for (int u : comp.part0) label[u] = '2';
            for (int u : comp.part1) label[u] = col;
        } else {
            for (int u : comp.part1) label[u] = '2';
            for (int u : comp.part0) label[u] = col;
        }
    }

    cout << "YES\n";
    for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) cout << label[i];
    cout << "\n";
    return 0;

x3=(4x1)/(4 - q²),

x4=(q/2)x3=(q/2)*(4x1)/(4 - q²)= (2 q x1)/(4 - q²)

Let’s compute for q=√5 -1. Let's compute q²:

q=√5 -1≈1.236,

q²=(√5 -1)^2=5 -2√5 +1=6 -2√5≈6 -4.472≈1.528.

Then 4 - q²≈4 -1.528≈2.472.

x3=4x1/(4 - q²)=4x1/2.472≈1.618x1,

x4=2q x1/(4 - q²)=2*(√5 -1)x1/2.472≈(2*1.236)/2.472≈2.472/2.472=1x1. So x4≈x1.

Therefore, the ratios are x1, x2=(2/q)x1≈(2/1.236)x1≈1.618x1, x3≈1.618x1, x4≈x1.

Thus, let's set x1=1, then x2≈1.618, x3≈1.618, x4≈1.

Compute LHS: 1² + (1.618)^2 + (1.618)^2 +1²≈1 +2.618 +2.618 +1≈7.236.

RHS: q(x1x2 +x2x3 +x3x4)= (√5 -1)[1*1.618 +1.618*1.618 +1.618*1] ≈ (1.236)[1.618 +2.618 +1.618]≈1.236*(5.854)≈7.236.

Thus, LHS - RHS≈7.236 -7.236=0. So at q=√5 -1, there exists a non-trivial solution where LHS - RHS=0. Therefore, if q exceeds this value, then for this specific x_i, LHS - RHS would be negative, violating the inequality. Therefore, the maximal q is indeed √5 -1. But earlier, when setting all variables equal, we saw that q can be up to 4/3≈1.333, which is larger than √5 -1≈1.236. However, since there exists a case (the Lagrange multipliers solution) where q=√5 -1 makes the expression zero, but for q higher than that, the expression would become negative for that case. Therefore, the maximum allowable q is √5 -1. Hence, the answer should be q ≤ √5 -1.

But let's check q=4/3≈1.333. Let's compute LHS - RHS for the case found via Lagrange multipliers when q=4/3.

Given that for q=4/3, let's compute the ratios:

x2=(2/q)x1= (2/(4/3))x1= (3/2)x1,

q²=16/9,

4 - q²=4 -16/9=20/9≈2.222,

x3=(4x1)/(4 - q²)= (4x1)/(20/9)= (36x1)/20= (9x1)/5≈1.8x1,

x4=(q/2)x3=(4/3 /2)*x3=(2/3)x3=(2/3)*(9x1/5)= (6x1)/5=1.2x1.

So x1=1, x2=1.5, x3=1.8, x4=1.2.

Compute LHS:1² +1.5² +1.8² +1.2²=1 +2.25 +3.24 +1.44=7.93.

RHS: (4/3)(1*1.5 +1.5*1.8 +1.8*1.2)= (4/3)(1.5 +2.7 +2.16)= (4/3)(6.36)=8.48.

So LHS - RHS=7.93 -8.48≈-0.55<0. Therefore, when q=4/3, there exists x_i such that LHS -RHS <0, hence violating the inequality. Therefore, the maximum q cannot be 4/3. So our initial test case was flawed because it suggested q=4/3, but in reality, when q=4/3, the inequality fails for some x_i (like the Lagrange multiplier case). Therefore, the correct maximum q is indeed √5 -1, which is approximately 1.236.

Therefore, the answer is q ≤ √5 -1, which is approximately 1.236. So all real numbers q such that q ≤ √5 -1 satisfy the inequality for all real x1, x2, x3, x4.

Thus, the answer is q ≤ √5 -1. To verify, let's compute √5 -1≈2.236 -1=1.236. So the maximum q is √5 -1, and all q less than or equal to that are valid.

**Final Answer**
The set of all real numbers \( q \) is \( q \leq \sqrt{5} - 1 \). Therefore, the answer is \(\boxed{\sqrt{5} - 1}\).
</think>To find all real numbers \( q \) for which the inequality
\[ x_1^2 + x_2^2 + x_3^2 + x_4^2 \ge q(x_1x_2 + x_2x_3 + x_3x_4) \]
holds for all real numbers \( x_1, x_2, x_3, x_4 \), we start by rewriting the inequality as a quadratic form:
\[ x_1^2 + x_2^2 + x_3^2 + x_4^2 - q(x_1x_2 + x_2x_3 + x_3x_4) \ge 0. \]

We consider the corresponding symmetric matrix \( M \) for this quadratic form:
\[
M = \begin{bmatrix}
1 & -\frac{q}{2} & 0 & 0 \\
-\frac{q}{2} & 1 & -\frac{q}{2} & 0 \\
0 & -\frac{q}{2} & 1 & -\frac{q}{2} \\
0 & 0 & -\frac{q}{2} & 1
\end{bmatrix}.
\]

To ensure the quadratic form is non-negative for all real vectors, the matrix \( M \) must be positive semi-definite. This requires all eigenvalues of \( M \) to be non-negative. Instead of calculating eigenvalues directly, we use an alternative approach involving Lagrange multipliers and critical points.

We set up the function:
\[ f(x_1, x_2, x_3, x_4) = x_1^2 + x_2^2 + x_3^2 + x_4^2 - q(x_1x_2 + x_2x_3 + x_3x_4) \]
and solve the system of equations derived from setting the gradient to zero:
\[
\begin{cases}
2x_1 - qx_2 = 0, \\
2x_2 - qx_1 - qx_3 = 0, \\
2x_3 - qx_2 - qx_4 = 0, \\
2x_4 - qx_3 = 0.
\end{cases}
\]

From the first equation, we have:
\[ x_2 = \frac{2x_1}{q}. \]

Substituting \( x_2 \) into the second equation:
\[ 2 \left( \frac{2x_1}{q} \right) - qx_1 - qx_3 = 0 \implies \frac{4x_1}{q} - qx_1 - qx_3 = 0 \implies x_3 = \frac{4x_1 - q^2 x_1}{q^2} = \frac{x_1 (4 - q^2)}{q^2}. \]

Substituting \( x_3 \) into the third equation:
\[ 2 \left( \frac{x_1 (4 - q^2)}{q^2} \right) - qx_2 - qx_4 = 0 \<#sep#> (all ways of arranging freezes and their positions)， but this may be too computationally heavy.

 full_towers = ns // 2<#sep#>Explicit attributes for supporting objects
Currently, supporting objects look for their attributes first in their own metadata, then in the metadata of the metadata of the parent. This poses some introspection ugliness, as it is difficult to determine what attributes are on the object.

Each supporting object should have its attributes explicitly stated and stored. This should improve performance and usability.

Maybe use `__slots__` as well?

#include <iostream>
#include <vector>
#include <string>
#include <queue>
#include <climits>

using namespace std;

vector<int> get_lucky_numbers(int n) {
    vector<int> lucky;
    queue<string> q;
    q.push("4");
    q.push("7");
    while (!q.empty()) {
        string s = q.front();
        q.pop();
        int num = stoi(s);
        if (num > n) continue;
        lucky.push_back(num);
        q.push(s + "4");
        q.push(s + "7");
    }
    return lucky;
}

vector<int> get_components(int n, const vector<pair<int, int>>& edges) {
    vector<vector<int>> adj(n + 1);
    for (auto [u, v] : edges) {
        adj[u].push_back(v);
        adj[v].push_back(u);
    }
    vector<bool> visited(n + 1, false);
    vector<int> sizes;
    for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {
        if (!visited[i]) {
            queue<int> q;
            q.push(i);
            visited[i] = true;
            int size = 0;
            while (!q.empty()) {
                int u = q.front();
                q.pop();
                size++;
                for (int v : adj[u]) {
                    if (!visited[v]) {
                        visited[v] = true;
                        q.push(v);
                    }
                }
            }
            sizes.push_back(size);
        }
    }
    return sizes;
}

bool is_lucky(int x) {
    string s = to_string(x);
    for (char c : s) {
        if (c != '4' && c != '7') return false;
    }
    return true;
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n, m;
    cin >> n >> m;
    vector<pair<int, int>> edges;
    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        int u, v;
        cin >> u >> v;
        edges.emplace_back(u, v);
    }

    vector<int> sizes = get_components(n, edges);

    for (int s : sizes) {
        if (is_lucky(s)) {
            cout << "0\n";
            return 0;
        }
    }

    vector<int> lucky = get_lucky_numbers(n);
    if (lucky.empty()) {
        cout << "-1\n";
        return 0;
    }

    vector<int> dp(n + 1, INT_MAX);
    dp[0] = 0;
    for (int x : sizes) {
        for (int s = n; s >= x; --s) {
            if (dp[s - x] != INT_MAX) {
                dp[s] = min(dp[s], dp[s - x] + 1);
            }
        }
    }

    int min_roads = INT_MAX;
    for (int s : lucky) {
        if (dp[s] != INT_MAX) {
            min_roads = min(min_roads, dp[s] - 1);
        }
    }

    if (min_roads == INT_MAX) {
        cout << "-1\n";
    } else {
        cout << min_roads << "\n";
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#> qx_3 = 0 \implies \frac{4x_1}{q} - qx_1 - qx_3 = 0 \implies x_3 = \frac{4x_1 - q^2 x_1}{q^2} = \frac{x_1 (4 - q^2)}{q^2}. \]

Substituting \( x_3 \) into the third equation:
\[ 2 \left( \frac{x_1 (4 - q^2)}{q^2} \right) - qx_2 - qx_4 = 0 \implies \frac{2x_1 (4 - q^2)}{q^2} - q \left( \frac{2x_1}{q} \right) - qx_4 = 0 \implies \frac{2x_1 (4 - q^2)}{q^2} - 2x_1 - qx_4 = 0 \implies x_4 = \frac{2x_1 (4 - q^2) - 2q^2 x_1}{q^3} = \frac{2x_1 (4 - 2q^2)}{q^3}. \]

Finally, substituting \( x_4 \) into the fourth equation:
\[ 2x_4 - qx_3 = 0 \implies 2 \left( \frac{2x_1 (4 - 2q^2)}{q^3} \right) - q \left( \frac{x_1 (4 - q^2)}{q^2} \right) = 0 \implies \frac{4x_1 (4 - 2q^2)}{q^3} - \frac{qx_1 (4 - q^2)}{q^2} = 0 \implies \frac{4x_1 (4 - 2q^2) - qx_1 (4 - q^2)}{q^3} = 0 \implies 4 (4 - 2q^2) - q (4 - q^2) = 0 \implies 16 - 8q^2 - 4q + q^3 = 0 \implies q^4 - 12q^2 + 16 = 0. \]

Let \( y = q^2 \). Then the equation becomes:
\[ y^2 - 12y + 16 = 0. \]

Solving this quadratic equation:
\[ y = \frac{12 \pm \sqrt{144 - 64}}{2} = \frac{12 \pm \sqrt{80}}{2} = \frac{12 \pm 4\sqrt{5}}{2} = 6 \pm 2\sqrt{5}. \]

Thus, \( q^2 = 6 + 2\sqrt{5} \) or \( q^2 = 6 - 2\sqrt{5} \). The relevant positive solutions are:
\[ q = \sqrt{6 + 2\sqrt{5}} \quad \text{and} \quad q = \sqrt{6 - 2\sqrt{5}}. \]

We need to determine which of these values is the maximum \( q \) ensuring the inequality holds for all real \( x_1, x_2, x_3, x_4 \). Testing these values, we find that the maximum \( q \) is \( \sqrt{5} - 1 \).

Therefore, the set of all real numbers \( q \) is \( q \leq \sqrt{5} - 1 \). The final answer is:
\[
\boxed{\sqrt{5} - 1}

2. Once the freeze_positions和 their counts are determined, compute the K function for all x.

 but 实際上, the integral is over all 实数x. Thus need to calculate the integral over the INTERVAL where each x is covered by the fire / electric and freeze 塔.

 To compute this accurately, there’s a need to represent the effective damage over x.

 this is possible通过 the following:

 the freeze count K(x) is the number of freezing塔 whose coverage includes x.

 Each freeze塔 is positioned at some x_coord ( as placed above), 在该 x_coord处, the coverage is:

 该 freeze tower is located at (x, y)是 either +1 или-1,但 it's centers is at x因此, the freeze塔在 x_塔的 coverage interval是 [ x_塔 - L_f, x_塔 + L_f], 其中 L_f是 sqrt(rs^2 -1).

 thus, for each freeze塔 at x_c:

 its interval is [x_c - Lฟ, x_c + L여].

 Hence the freeze count K(x)是 the number of freeze塔 such that x is in any of their intervals.

 Thus, the integral can be computed as follows:

我们 need compute the intergral over all x of [ (fire和 electric contributions) multiplied by (1 + K(x)))]

为了计算 this efficiently:

首先, collect all the intervals contribute d by每个 freeze塔的 their coverage.

 Each freeze塔 contributes 1 to K(x)在其 interval.

从而 the total K(x)是 the number of重叠 overlapping sections of their intervals.

 To calculate this, you can represent all freezing intervals and compute the count at each point x.

但是 since it's an integral over all x, we need to find the intervals where the freeze count is constant.

 Thus：

排序 all the intervals (每个 freeze塔的区间), then merge overlapping intervals and compute the count变化.

这 can be done by the sweep line algorithm:

 all the interval starts and ends can be stored as events, then process them in order, tracking the current K(-别名_ count).

 Then for each x 范囲 between two events can compute the K(count).

从而 the overall K(x) function can be broken into intervals with constant K.

 having the K(x) function broken down, we can then compute the contribution of fire and electric towers by overlaying their intervals.

 for each fire塔 placed at x_f, their coverage interval is [x_f -L_f, x_f+ L_f], where L_f is sqrt(rf^2-1).

 their contribution is df multiplied by the integral over this interval of (1 + K(x)) dx.

 this integral can be computed by checking the overlaps between their interval and the K(x) intervals.

 similarly for each electric塔: their contribution is de multiplied by the integral of (1 + K(x)) over their interval.

故 the total damage is the sum of all these integrals for all fire and electric towers.

 now, the challenge is to:

1. determine the optimal freeze pine和 their positions, then compute the maximum contribution from fire和 electric.

 the best position for each fire/涂子塔 would be 中心 of the freeze block's highest K region.

 thus, for each fire tower available:

 place it at the x position where the integral over its coverage of (1+K(x)) is maximized.

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

vector<int> solve(int n, int k, const vector<vector<int>>& constraints) {
    vector<vector<bool>> adj(n, vector<bool>(n, false));
    for (const auto& c : constraints) {
        const auto& vars = c;
        int m = vars.size();
        for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
            for (int j = i + 1; j < m; ++j) {
                int u = vars[i] - 1;
                int v = vars[j] - 1;
                adj[u][v] = true;
                adj[v][u] = true;
            }
        }
    }

    int max_clique = 0;
    for (int mask = 1; mask < (1 << n); ++mask) {
        vector<int> nodes;
        for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
            if (mask & (1 << i)) nodes.push_back(i);
        }
        bool is_clique = true;
        for (int i = 0; i < nodes.size(); ++i) {
            for (int j = i+1; j < nodes.size(); ++j) {
                if (!adj[nodes[i]][nodes[j]]) {
                    is_clique = false;
                    break;
                }
            }
            if (!is_clique) break;
        }
        if (is_clique) max_clique = max(max_clique, (int)nodes.size());
    }

    int m = max_clique;
    while (true) {
        vector<int> color(n, 0);
        function<bool(int)> backtrack = [&](int var) {
            if (var == n) return true;
            for (int c = 1; c <= m; ++c) {
                bool ok = true;
                for (int j = 0; j < var; ++j) {
                    if (adj[j][var] && color[j] == c) {
                        ok = false;
                        break;
                    }
                }
                if (ok) {
                    color[var] = c;
                    if (backtrack(var + 1)) return true;
                    color[var] = 0;
                }
            }
            return false;
        };
        if (backtrack(0)) break;
        m++;
    }

    vector<int> result(n, 0);
    bool found = false;
    vector<int> color(n, 0);
    function<void(int)> backtrack_lex = [&](int var) {
        if (found) return;
        if (var == n) {
            found = true;
            result = color;
            return;
        }
        for (int c = 1; c <= m; ++c) {
            bool ok = true;
            for (int j = 0; j < var; ++j) {
                if (adj[j][var] && color[j] == c) {
                    ok = false;
                    break;
                }
            }
            if (ok) {
                color[var] = c;
                backtrack_lex(var + 1);
                color[var] = 0;
            }
        }
    };
    backtrack_lex(0);

    return result;
}

int main() {
    int n, k;
    cin >> n >> k;
    vector<vector<int>> constraints;
    for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
        int m;
        cin >> m;
        vector<int> vars(m);
        for (int j = 0; j < m; ++j) cin >> vars[j];
        constraints.push_back(vars);
    }
    vector<int> res = solve(n, k, constraints);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        if (i > 0) cout << ' ';
        cout << res[i];
    }
    cout << endl;

由此, their coverage intervals would overlap maximum with the freeze's<#sep#>BUG ResultsReader drops last digit of scalar data
## Summary of issue
When reading a result file, we expect scalar data to have a space between the last digit and the colon due to
https://github.com/CORE-GATECH-GROUP/serpent-tools/blob/dd886ff2b2cd19e8dc681245134cef8815d61f31/serpentTools/utils/core.py#L14 and https://github.com/CORE-GATECH-GROUP/serpent-tools/blob/dd886ff2b2cd19e8dc681245134cef8815d61f31/serpentTools/parsers/results.py#L280-L282

The `- 2` was done to remove the colon and the trailing space. However, there are some values that don't have this trailing space
https://github.com/CORE-GATECH-GROUP/serpent-tools/blob/a69471eb211a18768aa55c695be906e006b4b4a6/data/pwr_res.m#L110-L118

## Code for reproducing the issue
```python
>>> from serpentTools.data import readDataFile
>>> res = readDataFile("pwr_res.m")  # read the above data file
>>> res.resdata["miscMemsize"]
array([[6.5], [6.5]])
```

## Actual outcome including console output and error traceback if applicable
See above
## Expected outcome
For this value, we should see `miscMemsize` give us `[[6.59], [6.59]]`

## Versions

Please provide the following:

* Version  from ``serpentTools.__version__`` `0.10.0.dev0` and `0.9.3`
* Python version - ``python --version`` `3.8`

## Potential fix
Improving the flexibility of the regular expressions. Possibly even using a match group to present the string / scalar / vector data in `match.groups()` rather than relying on `match.span`

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct Query {
    int l, r, k, idx;
    bool operator<(const Query& other) const {
        int block_size = 300; // Adjust block size based on constraints
        if (l / block_size != other.l / block_size) {
            return l < other.l;
        } else {
            return (l / block_size % 2 == 0) ? (r < other.r) : (r > other.r);
        }
    }
};

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(NULL);

    int n, m;
    cin >> n >> m;
    vector<int> a(n + 1); // 1-based indexing
    for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {
        cin >> a[i];
    }

    vector<Query> queries;
    vector<int> answers;
    vector<pair<int, int>> updates;
    int answer_idx = 0;

    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        int type;
        cin >> type;
        if (type == 1) {
            int l, r, k;
            cin >> l >> r >> k;
            queries.push_back({l, r, k, answer_idx++});
        } else {
            int p, x;
            cin >> p >> x;
            updates.emplace_back(p, x);
            answers.push_back(-2); // Placeholder for update queries
        }
    }

    // Process Mo's algorithm for type 1 queries
    sort(queries.begin(), queries.end());

    int current_l = 1, current_r = 0;
    unordered_map<int, int> freq;
    multiset<int> sorted_counts;
    unordered_map<int, int> cnt_freq; // Frequency of each count
    int max_cnt_freq = 0;

    auto add = [&](int pos) {
        int x = a[pos];
        if (freq[x] > 0) {
            auto it = sorted_counts.find(freq[x]);
            if (it != sorted_counts.end()) {
                sorted_counts.erase(it);
            }
            cnt_freq[freq[x]]--;
            if (cnt_freq[freq[x]] == 0) {
                cnt_freq.erase(freq[x]);
            }
        }
        freq[x]++;
        sorted_counts.insert(freq[x]);
        cnt_freq[freq[x]]++;
        if (cnt_freq[freq[x]] > max_cnt_freq) {
            max_cnt_freq = cnt_freq[freq[x]];
        }
    };

    auto remove = [&](int pos) {
        int x = a[pos];
        auto it = sorted_counts.find(freq[x]);
        if (it != sorted_counts.end()) {
            sorted_counts.erase(it);
        }
        cnt_freq[freq[x]]--;
        if (cnt_freq[freq[x]] == 0) {
            cnt_freq.erase(freq[x]);
        }
        freq[x]--;
        if (freq[x] > 0) {
            sorted_counts.insert(freq[x]);
            cnt_freq[freq[x]]++;
            if (cnt_freq[freq[x]] > max_cnt_freq) {
                max_cnt_freq = cnt_freq[freq[x]];
            }
        }
        // Update max_cnt_freq if the previous max was decremented
        if (freq[x] + 1 == max_cnt_freq && cnt_freq[freq[x] + 1] == 0) {
            max_cnt_freq = 0;
            for (auto& p : cnt_freq) {
                if (p.second > max_cnt_freq) {
                    max_cnt_freq = p.second;
                }
            }
        }
    };

    vector<int> final_answers(answer_idx, -1);

    for (auto& query : queries) {
        int l = query.l, r = query.r, k = query.k, idx = query.idx;
        while (current_l > l) add(--current_l);
        while (current_r < r) add(++current_r);
        while (current_l < l) remove(current_l++);
        while (current_r > r) remove(current_r--);

        int distinct = sorted_counts.size();
        if (distinct < k) {
            final_answers[idx] = -1;
            continue;
        }
        if (max_cnt_freq >= k) {
            final_answers[idx] = 0;
            continue;
        }

        vector<int> counts(sorted_counts.begin(), sorted_counts.end());
        int min_diff = INT_MAX;
        for (int i = 0; i + k - 1 < counts.size(); ++i) {
            min_diff = min(min_diff, counts[i + k - 1] - counts[i]);
        }
        final_answers[idx] = min_diff;
    }

    // Output the answers in the original order
    int type1_idx = 0;
    for (int ans : answers) {
        if (ans == -2) {
            // This is an update query, skip
            continue;
        } else {
            cout << final_answers[type1_idx++] << '\n';
        }
    }

    return 0;

Alternatively<#sep#>human: Inspired by this blog post.
Write a program that outputs 99 distinct programs (in the same language) that output the string I love you.
How the programs are separated from one another in the output will be defined by you. However, Each byte of output can only belong to at most 1 of the 99 programs. 
Constraints for Output Programs

If any string of characters is removed, then the program must not output I love you.
e.g. console.log('I love you');;; is invalid because ;;; can be removed
If any two strings of characters are both removed, then the program must not output I love you. This is to prevent inconsequential application of pairs of characters, that pass rule 1 because removing any single string will break the program.
e.g. print((('I love you'))) is invalid, because (( and )) can be removed

There will be two categories of scoring to participate in.

Standard code golf—smallest source code wins
Combined code golf—smallest source code + output wins

Leaderboard
Here is a Stack Snippet to generate both a regular leaderboard and an overview of winners by language.


/* Configuration */

var QUESTION_ID = 198052; // Obtain this from the url
// It will be like https://XYZ.stackexchange.com/questions/QUESTION_ID/... on any question page
var ANSWER_FILTER = "!t)IWYnsLAZle2tQ3KqrVveCRJfxcRLe";
var COMMENT_FILTER = "!)Q2B_A2kjfAiU78X(md6BoYk";
var OVERRIDE_USER = 48934; // This should be the user ID of the challenge author.

/* App */

var answers = [], answers_hash, answer_ids, answer_page = 1, more_answers = true, comment_page;

function answersUrl(index) {
  return "https://api.stackexchange.com/2.2/questions/" +  QUESTION_ID + "/answers?page=" + index + "&pagesize=100&order=desc&sort=creation&site=codegolf&filter=" + ANSWER_FILTER;
}

function commentUrl(index, answers) {
  return "https://api.stackexchange.com/2.2/answers/" + answers.join(';') + "/comments?page=" + index + "&pagesize=100&order=desc&sort=creation&site=codegolf&filter=" + COMMENT_FILTER;
}

function getAnswers() {
  jQuery.ajax({
    url: answersUrl(answer_page++),
    method: "get",
    dataType: "jsonp",
    crossDomain: true,
    success: function (data) {
      answers.push.apply(answers, data.items);
      answers_hash = [];
      answer_ids = [];
      data.items.forEach(function(a) {
        a.comments = [];
        var id = +a.share_link.match(/\d+/);
        answer_ids.push(id);
        answers_hash[id] = a;
      });
      if (!data.has_more) more_answers = false;
      comment_page = 1;
      getComments();
    }
  });
}

function getComments() {
  jQuery.ajax({
    url: commentUrl(comment_page++, answer_ids),
    method: "get",
    dataType: "jsonp",
    crossDomain: true,
    success: function (data) {
      data.items.forEach(function(c) {
        if (c.owner.user_id === OVERRIDE_USER)
          answers_hash[c.post_id].comments.push(c);
      });
      if (data.has_more) getComments();
      else if (more_answers) getAnswers();
      else process();
    }
  });  
}

getAnswers();

var SCORE_REG = /<h\d>\s*([^\n,]*[^\s,]),.*?(\d+)(?=[^\n\d<>]*(?:<(?:s>[^\n<>]*<\/s>|[^\n<>]+>)[^\n\d<>]*)*<\/h\d>)/;

var OVERRIDE_REG = /^Override\s*header:\s*/i;

function getAuthorName(a) {
  return a.owner.display_name;
}

function process() {
  var valid = [];
  
  answers.forEach(function(a) {
    var body = a.body;
    a.comments.forEach(function(c) {
      if(OVERRIDE_REG.test(c.body))
        body = '<h1>' + c.body.replace(OVERRIDE_REG, '') + '</h1>';
    });
    
    var match = body.match(SCORE_REG);
    if (match)
      valid.push({
        user: getAuthorName(a),
        size: +match[2],
        language: match[1],
        link: a.share_link,
      });
    
  });
  
  valid.sort(function (a, b) {
    var aB = a.size,
        bB = b.size;
    return aB - bB
  });

  var languages = {};
  var place = 1;
  var lastSize = null;
  var lastPlace = 1;
  valid.forEach(function (a) {
    if (a.size != lastSize)
      lastPlace = place;
    lastSize = a.size;
    ++place;
    
    var answer = jQuery("#answer-template").html();
    answer = answer.replace("{{PLACE}}", lastPlace + ".")
                   .replace("{{NAME}}", a.user)
                   .replace("{{LANGUAGE}}", a.language)
                   .replace("{{SIZE}}", a.size)
                   .replace("{{LINK}}", a.link);
    answer = jQuery(answer);
    jQuery("#answers").append(answer);

    var lang = a.language;
    if (/<a/.test(lang)) lang = jQuery(lang).text();
    
    languages[lang] = languages[lang] || {lang: a.language, user: a.user, size: a.size, link: a.link};
  });

  var langs = [];
  for (var lang in languages)
    if (languages.hasOwnProperty(lang))
      langs.push(languages[lang]);

  langs.sort(function (a, b) {
    if (a.lang > b.lang) return 1;
    if (a.lang < b.lang) return -1;
    return 0;
  });

  for (var i = 0; i < langs.length; ++i)
  {
    var language = jQuery("#language-template").html();
    var lang = langs[i];
    language = language.replace("{{LANGUAGE}}", lang.lang)
                       .replace("{{NAME}}", lang.user)
                       .replace("{{SIZE}}", lang.size)
                       .replace("{{LINK}}", lang.link);
    language = jQuery(language);
    jQuery("#languages").append(language);
  }

}

gpt: <think> Okay, let's tackle this problem. We need to write a program that outputs 99 distinct programs in the same language, each of which outputs "I love you". The tricky part is that each byte in our output can only belong to one of these programs, and the generated programs must adhere to specific constraints. 

First, the constraints for the output programs: They can't have any parts that can be removed without breaking their ability to output the string. Also, removing exactly two strings (like a pair) of characters shouldn't allow the program<#sep#> from numpy import array, ndarray, fromiter
 
 # Regular expressions
 
-STR_REGEX = compile(r'\'.+\'')  # string
-VEC_REGEX = compile(r'(?<==.)\[.+?\]')  # vector
-SCALAR_REGEX = compile(r'=.+;')  # scalar
+STR_REGEX = compile(r"([A-Z_]+).*'(.*)'")

#include <iostream>
#include <vector>
#include <string>
#include <memory>
#include <cctype>
#include <sstream>
#include <cassert>

using namespace std;

class Expr {
public:
    virtual int evaluate(int n, const vector<int>& memo) const = 0;
    virtual ~Expr() = default;
};

class NumberExpr : public Expr {
    int value;
public:
    NumberExpr(int v) : value(v) {}
    int evaluate(int, const vector<int>&) const override { return value; }
};

class VariableNExpr : public Expr {
public:
    int evaluate(int n, const vector<int>&) const override { return n; }
};

class SumExpr : public Expr {
    unique_ptr<Expr> left, right;
    char op;
public:
    SumExpr(unique_ptr<Expr> l, unique_ptr<Expr> r, char o) : left(move(l)), right(move(r)), op(o) {}
    int evaluate(int n, const vector<int>& memo) const override {
        int lv = left->evaluate(n, memo);
        int rv = right->evaluate(n, memo);
        if (op == '+') return (lv + rv) % 32768;
        else return (lv - rv + 32768) % 32768;
    }
};

class ProductExpr : public Expr {
    unique_ptr<Expr> left, right;
    char op;
public:
    ProductExpr(unique_ptr<Expr> l, unique_ptr<Expr> r, char o) : left(move(l)), right(move(r)), op(o) {}
    int evaluate(int n, const vector<int>& memo) const override {
        int lv = left->evaluate(n, memo);
        int rv = right->evaluate(n, memo);
        if (op == '*') return (lv * rv) % 32768;
        else return lv / rv;
    }
};

class FunctionCallExpr : public Expr {
    unique_ptr<Expr> arg;
public:
    FunctionCallExpr(unique_ptr<Expr> a) : arg(move(a)) {}
    int evaluate(int n, const vector<int>& memo) const override {
        int k = arg->evaluate(n, memo);
        return memo[k];
    }
};

class Statement {
public:
    virtual int evaluate(int n, const vector<int>& memo) const = 0;
    virtual ~Statement() = default;
};

class ReturnStatement : public Statement {
    unique_ptr<Expr> expr;
public:
    ReturnStatement(unique_ptr<Expr> e) : expr(move(e)) {}
    int evaluate(int n, const vector<int>& memo) const override {
        return expr->evaluate(n, memo);
    }
};

class IfStatement : public Statement {
    unique_ptr<Expr> left, right, retExpr;
    string op;
public:
    IfStatement(unique_ptr<Expr> l, string o, unique_ptr<Expr> r, unique_ptr<Expr> re) 
        : left(move(l)), op(move(o)), right(move(r)), retExpr(move(re)) {}
    int evaluate(int n, const vector<int>& memo) const override {
        int lv = left->evaluate(n, memo);
        int rv = right->evaluate(n, memo);
        bool cond = false;
        if (op == ">") cond = (lv > rv);
        else if (op == "<") cond = (lv < rv);
        else if (op == "==") cond = (lv == rv);
        if (cond) return retExpr->evaluate(n, memo);
        else return -1;
    }
};

vector<string> tokenize(const string& code) {
    vector<string> tokens;
    size_t i = 0;
    while (i < code.size()) {
        while (i < code.size() && isspace(code[i])) i++;
        if (i >= code.size()) break;
        if (isalpha(code[i])) {
            string tok;
            while (i < code.size() && isalnum(code[i])) tok += code[i++];
            tokens.push_back(tok);
        } else if (isdigit(code[i])) {
            string num;
            while (i < code.size() && isdigit(code[i])) num += code[i++];
            tokens.push_back(num);
        } else if (code[i] == '=' && i+1 < code.size() && code[i+1] == '=') {
            tokens.push_back("==");
            i += 2;
        } else {
            tokens.push_back(string(1, code[i++]));
        }
    }
    return tokens;
}

class Parser {
    vector<string> tokens;
    size_t pos;
public:
    Parser(vector<string> t) : tokens(t), pos(0) {}
    
    unique_ptr<Expr> parseArithExpr() { return parseSum(); }
    
    unique_ptr<Expr> parseSum() {
        auto left = parseProduct();
        while (pos < tokens.size()) {
            string t = tokens[pos];
            if (t == "+" || t == "-") {
                pos++;
                auto right = parseProduct();
                left = make_unique<SumExpr>(move(left), move(right), t[0]);
            } else break;
        }
        return left;
    }
    
    unique_ptr<Expr> parseProduct() {
        auto left = parseMultiplier();
        while (pos < tokens.size()) {
            string t = tokens[pos];
            if (t == "*" || t == "/") {
                pos++;
                auto right = parseMultiplier();
                left = make_unique<ProductExpr>(move(left), move(right), t[0]);
            } else break;
        }
        return left;
    }
    
    unique_ptr<Expr> parseMultiplier() {
        string t = tokens[pos++];
        if (t == "n") return make_unique<VariableNExpr>();
        else if (isdigit(t[0])) return make_unique<NumberExpr>(stoi(t));
        else if (t == "f") {
            assert(tokens[pos++] == "(");
            auto arg = parseArithExpr();
            assert(tokens[pos++] == ")");
            return make_unique<FunctionCallExpr>(move(arg));
        }
        cerr << "Unexpected token: " << t << endl;
        exit(1);
    }
    
    vector<unique_ptr<Statement>> parseStatements() {
        vector<unique_ptr<Statement>> stmts;
        while (pos < tokens.size()) {
            string t = tokens[pos];
            if (t == "if") {
                pos++;
                assert(tokens[pos++] == "(");
                auto left = parseArithExpr();
                string op = tokens[pos++];
                auto right = parseArithExpr();
                assert(tokens[pos++] == ")");
                assert(tokens[pos++] == "return");
                auto retExpr = parseArithExpr();
                assert(tokens[pos++] == ";");
                stmts.push_back(make_unique<IfStatement>(move(left), op, move(right), move(retExpr)));
            } else if (t == "return") {
                pos++;
                auto expr = parseArithExpr();
                assert(tokens[pos++] == ";");
                stmts.push_back(make_unique<ReturnStatement>(move(expr)));
            } else {
                cerr << "Unexpected token in statements: " << t << endl;
                exit(1);
            }
        }
        return stmts;
    }
};

int main() {
    int target;
    cin >> target;
    cin.ignore();
    
    string code, line;
    while (getline(cin, line)) code += line + "\n";
    
    size_t start = code.find('{');
    size_t end = code.find('}', start);
    string body = code.substr(start+1, end - start - 1);
    
    auto tokens = tokenize(body);
    Parser parser(tokens);
    auto statements = parser.parseStatements();
    
    vector<int> memo(32768, -1);
    for (int n = 0; n <= 32767; ++n) {
        for (const auto& stmt : statements) {
            int val = stmt->evaluate(n, memo);
            if (val != -1) {
                memo[n] = val;
                break;
            }
        }
    }
    
    for (int n = 32767; n >= 0; --n) {
        if (memo[n] == target) {
            cout << n << endl;
            return 0;
        }
    }
    cout << -1 << endl;
    return 0;
